# D3 Task 52 (EEDCS-52): host numeric half of the FIXED-object target point and its branch-3 replay (2 November 2026): SOURCE + 21/21 FOCUSED HOST UNITS, FMT AND BOTH STRICT CLIPPY PASS ON THE WORKING TREE; NO RETAIL PARITY, NO CORE CUTOVER

`ModelStore::target_point_fixed(shape, base, heading)` (Lua `target_point_fixed`) and `eech_model_fixed_target_point` in `csrc/model_step.c` copy the original branch 3 of `get_local_entity_target_point` with its types and order under the existing floating-point guard: the shape's offset comes from the host's own model; x and z are added only when the offset has a horizontal part AND the heading is non-zero (the legacy quirk kept: a zero heading skips them), y is always added for a valid shape; sin and cos are the double functions assigned to float; no Rust or Lua arithmetic. The invalid shape 0 is the position copy (the position's own bits, no add of zero), distinct from a valid empty slot (adds y, so -0.0 becomes +0.0), from an out-of-catalogue shape (argument error) and from an inventory-only store (refuses). A heading is read only where the original reads it; a needed heading that is absent is `heading_missing`, never a default. `compare_target_points` now replays every recorded branch-3 query from the exact recorded `base` and `heading` with the host-derived offset and the original `result` as oracle, and counts matched, mismatched, refused and missing-input apart (an older report without `base` is missing its input, never a pass; the offset the original read is compared only as an input). The branch-1 and local-offset comparisons are unchanged.

Gates for this change, run on the WORKING TREE after Root's 95199 Linux receipt was terminal 0 and `docker ps` was empty (pinned image, dedicated WSL endpoint, one merged writer, log `d3-tp-fixed-wt-20261102-2232.log`): `model_target_point` 21/21 (13 earlier plus 8 new: no-horizontal short circuit, zero-heading quirk, `heading_missing`, tiny-heading rotation, shape 0 versus the valid empty slot, domain/inventory, floating-point state and rounding, Lua face with the branch-3 comparer counts), `target_point_characterization` 42/42, `cargo fmt --all --check` 0 after one formatting pass, strict clippy for `eech-engine`/`eech-dc`/`eech-world` all-targets 0 default and with `eech-dc/target-point-fixture`. The first run failed one earlier comparer test, only because its stand-in `branch=3` row was a minimal 'other branch' placeholder the new replay rightly rejects as incomplete; the row is now branch 2. These are source-level checks, not retail parity, authority or a named native build. Root runs the comparer on the new captures that record `base`. Core still owns the original initializer and queries, branches 2 and 4 with the instance-viewpoint write, `gd_attck` all-three-offset consumption and seeker/selection/guidance policy. No baseline, tolerance, oracle, input, seed, duration or scenario changed.

# D3 Task 52 (EEDCS-52): setup probe for the three fallback cases with no live candidate prepared (2 November 2026): FIXTURE + DRIVER + GUARDS, SOURCE ONLY, UNFORMATTED, UNBUILT, UNRUN, UNREVIEWED

State, as reported by Root and not re-read: at `75d6cb62a` the focused, default and fixture packages (30/30 each), fmt and BOTH strict exited 0, and the controlled run at `c500a314a7` completed for Georgia and Lebanon (native and controllers 0, four pre/post input manifests 0, 84 payloads; valid cohort scans; the read-only probe called the original for cases 2, 3 and 4, two picks each, viewpoint unchanged; cases 1, 5 and 6 UNAVAILABLE for lack of a live candidate). This step adds, without touching that read-only mode or its measured results, a distinct opt-in `probe=setup`: it gives a real live entity an explicit physical precondition (a real aircraft or vehicle's instance replaced by a temporarily constructed instance of a real census bounding-box scene with no skin; the instance pointer of a real mobile (possibly another one) NULL; a real fixed structure's shape set invalid through its typed setter), calls the ORIGINAL once, restores it and verifies the restoration (pointer, shape, the real instance's viewpoint words, the three construction counters) before the next case, all before any frame; any failed restoration refuses and stops the run. # D3 Task 52 (EEDCS-52): grammar-inventory guard and the probe refusal's error channel (2 November 2026): TEST ONLY, SOURCE ONLY, UNFORMATTED, UNBUILT, UNRUN, UNREVIEWED

That text is the probe's refusal: written once by `probe_refused`, returned as a non-zero status, turned by the engine entry into a bad argument and kept by `keep_case_reason` as the Lua error that the driver's checked call stops on; it is not a row of a successful report. Test-only adaptation: the inventory recognises that ONE exact shape (`tp-probe-refused`: `reason`, `visited`) as the error channel (exactly once, and the driver must not parse it), and a new guard verifies the channel itself (one write in `probe_refused`, never an `emit` into the report, `keep_case_reason` converting a non-empty bad-argument report into the error, the driver reaching the probe through its checked call and having no parser for the row); every other emitted `tp-` row is still required to match the driver both ways, the successful grammar is not weakened, and the behavioural refusal guards (failure, no frame, no query read) are unchanged. No production C/Rust/Lua change. Closed original review and publication stay valid; physical queries have not moved; branches 2/3/4 and probe actuals remain unmeasured; whole D3 open; D4/D5 unstarted; Task 50 untouched.

# D3 Task 52 (EEDCS-52): controlled-probe scan safety, accounting and the class-number drift guard (2 November 2026): FIXTURE + DRIVER + GUARDS, SOURCE ONLY, UNFORMATTED, UNBUILT, UNRUN, UNREVIEWED

Actual gates at `34a4bb3437`, preserved: the Windows build exited 0; the Linux default package command exited 101 solely on `the_driver_names_and_caps_are_the_emitters` (24 passed, 1 failed): the guard compared the emitter's `site_name` return literals in SOURCE order with the driver's caller-class table, but the C switch lists `controlled_probe` before the `default` that names `other`, while the numeric classes (other 5, controlled 6) were correct. # D3 Task 52 (EEDCS-52): query generations anchored to the sampled call boundaries (2 November 2026): LUA + GUARDS, SOURCE ONLY, UNFORMATTED, UNBUILT, UNRUN, UNREVIEWED

State at `7af39eca7384425c3d2f093def531d1b41df3a0b`: Root's immutable 7af Linux and Windows gates and the dependent first corrected-generation captures are still running; no result of them is claimed here and their evidence is not relabelled. The prior `7c0e592ee` default run failed only the partial-capture guard (fixed at `862fb482e`, a fixture field collision). The same independent reviewer then found one residual in the generation group: `event.gen` was joined only to the final ended passes and to the events' monotone order, so, for example, after a boot that ended two passes at zero calls a later branch-3 query tagged generation 0 with exactly pass 0's words was accepted, and a pre-boot query could name a pass added only during the frames. Source-only fix in the driver and its guards: each summary the driver reads (boot, sampled frames, end) bounds the queries by the observer's own semantics, `seq` being the call count at the query's BEGIN and `gen` the latest ended pass then (a query that began before a sample's call count cannot name a later pass than the sample's; one at or after it cannot name an earlier one); the open flag is not constrained, no pass count is forced, the latest ended pass may not go backwards between samples, and a boot query between passes plus a later third pass stay valid. New guards: a coherent positive and single-fault future pre-boot, stale post-sample and stale post-boot generations. No C observer, original statement, anchor or value changed. Not shown: that it compiles or passes. Task 52 and whole D3 stay open; production extraction is ahead; D4/D5 unstarted.

# D3 integration caller adaptation: the four model/route diagnostic drivers boot with the host environment provider (2 November 2026): LUA + TEST COLLABORATORS, SOURCE ONLY, UNBUILT, UNRUN, UNREVIEWED

The actual four-feature Windows build on `e9d77de8d` (build 0, 83 payloads) ran the first G/L model-animation controller: it stopped, native and controller 1, at the primary `[env-provider:missing]`. The campaign module is right to require the host's environment provider (Task51). The four diagnostic drivers that were introduced only on the Task50 parent boot the real module without it. This is a driver integration omission of the Task50 callers, not a C, model-kernel or profile defect; the reference host already supplies `host.env_provider ()` (with the sound catalogue and backend defaults) and the other drivers already pass it.

Change (4 Lua + 4 test files + this entry; production code, `model-characterization.lua`, `model-provider-check`, `model-host-compare` and the 19 selectors untouched):
- `lua/model-animation-characterization.lua`, `lua/model-horizon-control.lua`, `lua/route-characterization.lua`, `lua/model-authority.lua`: the boot configuration gains `environment = host.env_provider ()`; every other field, argument, protocol and named negative case is unchanged. In `model-authority.lua` the `missing` mode still gives no model, and the module checks the model provider before the environment one, so the intentional missing-model refusal keeps its precedence.
- Frozen, deliberately NOT changed: `lua/model-characterization.lua` (declared historical original-profile evidence, incompatible with the new module by design, reused at its old native identity). The missing-model and missing-terrain negative contracts stay.
- `tests/model_animation_characterization.rs`, `tests/model_horizon_control.rs`, `tests/route_characterization.rs` (fresh direct-Lua hosts): the scripted `host` gains an `env_provider` factory that returns a function, as the real one does; that function answers no physical question and raises by name (`the stand-in host answers no physical environment question: <op>`) if it is ever asked one. The scripted `boot` asserts `config.environment` is a function. All numerical, malformed, clock, capability and refusal assertions are unchanged.
- `tests/model_store.rs` (the `model-authority` stand-in runs through the REAL eech-world host, which already installs `env_provider`): no synthetic factory; the stand-in boot checks `config.environment` is a function with the module's `[env-provider:missing]` marker, placed AFTER the existing missing-model check.

Static checks only (no compiler, no run): each of the four drivers has exactly one boot configuration and now carries exactly one `environment = host.env_provider ()`; the edits add no `{`/`}` to the `format!` test collaborators; `host_callbacks.rs`' profile-selection counts are unaffected (no selector added or removed). # D3 integration test-adaptation, second part: the lightning queue stand-in builds its integer bit masks exactly (2 November 2026): TESTS ONLY, SOURCE ONLY, UNBUILT, UNRUN, UNREVIEWED

The actual Linux default suite on `e9d77de8d` (61 passed, 3 failed in `host_callbacks`) showed a second test defect beside the fifteen missing selector collaborators of `d6d708f76`. The queue stand-in `ENV_STAND_IN_FIXTURE` in `tests/host_callbacks.rs` built its 32-bit slot masks with the runtime exponentiation `2 ^ slot` (also in its popcount, lowest-free-slot and slot-row loops). Under the new Linux SSE round-toward-zero of the reference host that power is inexact, so the second add's mask rendered `00000002` instead of `00000003`; the healthy positive was malformed, and two negative cases that rewrite the literal `00000003` (`queue_wrong_plan`, `queue_final_unrecorded_slot`) became silent no-ops on it. This is a data-generator defect of the test, not a changed oracle: every expected mask, physical value, malformed-report pattern and refusal predicate is unchanged.

Correction (only `eech-campaign/crates/eech-world/tests/host_callbacks.rs` plus this entry; the fifteen files of `d6d708f76`, all production code and all drivers are untouched):
- The stand-in builds the bit weights `1, 2, 4 ... 2^31` once by exact doubling and uses one bounded accessor (0..31, anything else is a loud stand-in error) in all four places that used `2 ^ n`. Powers of two to 2^31, their sums by doubling and division of a 32-bit mask by them are exact in every rounding mode. No other arithmetic of the stand-in was altered.
- Every corruption named `queue_*` is now compared with the coherent report it was applied to; a no-op raises a distinctive stand-in error (`NO-OP MUTATION`), and the two Rust loops that rely on a refusal (the incomplete/out-of-order list and the natural slot-cohort list, which counts any failure as a refusal) first assert that this marker is absent. The coherent alternatives (`queue_no_environment`, `queue_full`, `queue_phantom_event`, `queue_missing_event`) differ from the healthy report of the shape they run with, so the same check applies to them. The sound and control forms are not touched.

Windows evidence that bears on the disposition (reported by Root, not mine): fresh tiny ordinary processes of old `78fe5efdb` and `e9`, no campaign boot, report `D:/EECH-Migration-Evidence/model-integrated-caller-profile-probe-20261102-1612/report.json`: ten `0.1` additions give `0.99999999999999989` after prepare on old78 vs `0.99999999999999978`; on e9 `...89` after prepare and `...78` only after the explicit profile selector, i.e. exactly the old behaviour; the Windows runtime power and the 2/3 masks are unchanged. This supports keeping the 19 production selectors; the Linux mask generator problem is platform-specific. It is a tiny-process observation, no full-MXCSR, x87 or campaign claim.

Static checks only (no compiler, no run): no `^` exponentiation or libm power remains in the stand-in; `host_callbacks.rs` is the only changed test file; the new Rust assertions are formatted for the 160-column rule; the new Lua is inside the raw string without `"#`. Whether the three failing tests now pass, and whether a stand-in error reaches the host's stderr as the marker check assumes, is for Root's canonical run on the corrected source. `e9d77de8d` stays the runtime identity of all Root binaries and actuals; they are not relabelled to this source. Task51 Done at `a42d86e75`; Task50 and whole D3 remain open; D4/D5 unstarted; physical target-point, hierarchy and collision services still required.

# D3 integration test-collaborator correction after the independent merge review of `e9d77de8d` (2 November 2026): TESTS ONLY, SOURCE ONLY, UNBUILT, UNRUN, UNREVIEWED

The independent changed-source review of the Task50/Task51 merge `e9d77de8dab85b1b1f504051a8b8ad9fb4c909a1` found ONE P2 group and no other source blocker: fifteen direct-Lua environment tests create their own scripted `host` but did not install `host.use_reference_arithmetic`, which the merged `env-*.lua` drivers call right after `dc.prepare_installation`. Their coherent positives would stop at a nil selector before the protocol they check. This entry corrects exactly those fifteen test files; no production source, driver, baseline, oracle, tolerance, input or flag changed, and `e9d77de8d` stays the runtime identity of everything Root builds and runs. The corrected tests are a later, separate source revision.

The test-only protocol is prepare -> select the reference arithmetic profile -> boot:
- Six files already record an `ORDER` list (`env_sound_native_guard`, `env_sound_handle_guard`, `env_sound_handle`, `env_sound_route`, `env_rotor_sounds`, `env_play_resumed_parent`, the last for both the original and the elided driver): the scripted selector records `"select"`, and the existing exact order assertions now read `prepare, select, arm:true, boot, ...` (the rotor prefix check is four steps), so selection is still verified to follow preparation and precede boot.
- Nine files kept no order list (`env_ambient_authority`, `env_lightning_queue`, `env_sector_authority`, `env_sector_characterization`, `env_sound_characterization`, `env_sound_init` for its three drivers, `env_sound_lifetime`, `env_sound_wide_children`, `env_wind_authority`): the scripted module now carries a small stage (`new` -> `prepared` -> `selected`); the selector raises unless the installation was just prepared and the scripted boot raises unless the profile was selected. A driver that skips, reorders or repeats the selection therefore fails.
- Every damaged-report, numerical and failure oracle and every existing positive and negative case is untouched; nothing was removed, weakened or made to swallow a nil error; there is no production fallback.

Deliberately NOT changed: `env_rotor_host_witness.rs` (its delegate is synthetic, not the changed rotor script), the real-host-executable `host_callbacks.rs` environment tests (the real host already installs the selector) and `terrain_sector.rs` (the driver is only mentioned in prose).

# D3 L2 handshake corrections after the 12a review (2 November 2026, source only)

Task50 / PR110. The review of `12a642d54e` cleared the carry-over correction and found three handshake groups; Root's strict lint on that source added a type-complexity refusal. This entry records the SOURCE corrections. NOTHING here is built or run; the static checks made (strict-count simulation of all 33 staged patches, bracket balance) are not compile or runtime evidence. The 12a default recordings Root collected (3025 scenes, literal against the retained original lists) stay at their own identity. Task50 and whole D3 stay open.

1. Bounded announcement. A provider's walk announcement is one transaction: at most 4096 files (the campaign's own limit), each (scene, directory) once, steps one after another from 0, every domain checked before any state changes, and a closed announcement is final (a late step, a second end or a new walk is refused; a new provider is a new transaction). Unit tests exercise the state machine with a small cap; real-VM witnesses exercise the lifecycle, the duplicate, the out-of-sequence step, the over-long name, the scene domain and the closed walk. The 4096 limit of the host and of the campaign are tied by a source test.

2. Traversal completion. The staged traversal now ends with a check that every announced position was opened, each either read by `read_scene` or explicitly SKIPPED, and none outstanding. The original's own skip is characterised in source: `read_custom_scene` reads a scene only for versions -1 to -3 and otherwise logs and returns (tests read the original and pin it); the skipped branch consumes its position as skipped, claims no row and asks the host nothing, and opening a file while another is neither read nor skipped stops by name.

3. Replay initialisation and the pinned installation. `host.model` no longer loads the sorted custom history first. The database's own questions (object count, database definitions) and the acceptance of the announcement come from an INVENTORY store that reads no custom scene file; the final store is built once, from the announced order, after the announcement closes; no final answer (scene, triangle, route, custom definition) is given before that, and a sorted store is built only when a tool asks with no announcement, after which an announcement is refused. A real-parser fixture of 1600 custom scenes shows a read order that fits the original's room of 2000 objects where the sorted order does not (the 1000-name history fills before a shared name), loading by inventory and by the announced order while the sorted load is refused by name. The installation is pinned as an absolute path when the provider is registered (or when first needed, if the argument was not there yet) and replay uses that path: a relative root, a changed working directory or a changed host argument selects nothing else; a witness runs with a relative root from another directory and a mutated argument.

4. The six-value callback tuple has a named type for the strict lint. The nine-field metadata counters, the exact carry-over, T2 through the original add_new_animation, the truthful names, the true-backing hooks and the reached controls are unchanged. Root owns every build, native run and review.

# D3 L2 phase corrections after the 32b review (2 November 2026, source only)

Task50 / PR110. The same review of `32b39b99c4` left two residual phase findings, and Root's live gates added a build warning and a strict-lint refusal. This entry records the SOURCE corrections. NOTHING here is built or run; the static checks made (strict-count simulation of all 31 staged patches, bracket balance) are not compile or runtime evidence. The literal comparison Root made of source 32's default recordings with the retained original lists (3025 scenes, 95 listed, 2742 empty, 188 absent, 510 ordered ids, literal) stands at its own identity and says nothing about these changes. Task50 and whole D3 stay open.

1. Definition identity and the real walk order. The original walks `OBJECTS` with `_findfirst` / `_findnext`, which here is the file system's own, unsorted, order (`eech_win32.c`), and what a custom definition sees depends on it: the number the first named object takes and the table object it replaces, the per-directory history of named objects, which definition of a scene is read last. Core keeps that walk unchanged and now ANNOUNCES it to the host before reading any file (a pre-walk of the same enumeration, one step per file: scene, position, directory name; `CUSTOM_ORDER`), verifies at each file it opens that it is the one announced at that position, and names a custom definition by that position. The host replays its definitions in the announced order (`load_in_order`, history reset per directory as the original does) and refuses a file the walk names that it lacks, names twice, or never reaches; a position of another scene is refused. A sorted walk is only the order when none is announced (tools). The distinguishing fixture is a scene whose object is named by hex number: the table's object 4 or the named object that replaced it, according to which file was walked first, shown by independent public views (scene bounds computed at read time) as well as by the headers. This is NOT a proof that the host's sorted order equals the campaign's; the host does not claim it.

2. Header carry-over. `read_object` assigns counts, bounds, radius and buffers and never `culling_normals_offset`. The host store keeps the row's value when an object is read into an existing row (binary-hex override; the first named object), and Core clears only what `read_object` assigns and remembers a described row it replaced; when the new object is described, the offset the row kept must equal the host's, or the engine stops (`[model-provider:object.carry]`). A real-parser fixture gives table objects non-zero offsets and checks the override, the first named object (carried) and the second (fresh, zero).

3. Counters. The metadata row has a ninth field, `rows_replaced` (a described row taken over by a new number): rows holding a description are `rows_filled - rows_replaced`; the horizon driver and its tests validate it. 4. Dead state removed: the scene's own approximation and shadow lists (the facts of each definition replaced them); no allow, no dummy read. The repeated override-name type is a named alias for the strict lint.

The 32b-built T2 comparison, the truthful names, the fifth-argument transport and the changed-control checks are unchanged. The unknown-name control can already resolve to 0 in the reference; it is no causal change until compared with a healthy run. Root owns every build, native run and review; fresh roots are Root's.

It keeps EVERY definition of each scene in the order the loader read them (the database record, then each custom scene file), each with the object facts as they stood when it was read; the campaign counts its own reads of a scene and asks by `(scene, definition, role, cursor)` (a new `definition` field and fifth provider argument, an ordinal of reads, no object number), and the kind it is reading must equal the host's or the answer is refused. 2. T2 compatibility restored. The host sends a truthful text name (valid text, no control characters, at most 127 bytes, upper-cased in ASCII; invalid bytes, empty names and file indices outside the compiled names are refused, never repaired, never sent as numbers). Core resolves it with the original `add_new_animation`, so a valid name its table lacks keeps the accepted tolerance (animation 0), exactly the retained row of scene 2826. The host no longer checks names against the installation. This supersedes the earlier L2 entry's declared difference on unknown names. The authority mode `animation_unknown` is no longer a refusal: it is a changed-host control whose consequence is the scene's final list beginning with 0.

3. The two `eech_model_request` initializers in `eech-engine/src/lib.rs` carry the new fields; a callback test now carries the object family, the table count and an animation name.

4. Changed-host controls. `animation_dropped` and `animation_unknown` are covered only when the change was actually given, the changed definition is the scene's final one and the module's own host-profile row shows the consequence (empty list; first entry 0). A run that never asked, or has no `model_host_animations`, fails as UNREACHED, never exit 0. Stand-in tests run the real authority script for the covered case, the not-asked case, the no-rows case and an engine that ignores the answers. They are not causal evidence until the Root runs them; the scene is the first the campaign's own load asked about, chosen by no one.

# D3 original animation-list driver: coherence correction (1 November 2026): UNBUILT, UNRUN

The independent review cleared the observer's C and hooks and found one driver and test coherence group, corrected in `lua/model-animation-characterization.lua` and its tests only; the C fixture, hooks,
production, model, route, kernel and public feature plumbing are exactly as at `4485`. The Root's 4485 builds are on their OWN source (Windows 0, Linux still running) and there is no native run of the
observer yet; the buffer cut at `abfda86326` stays finite-closed on its own actuals and is not whole Task50 or D3.

* **Headers joined to rows.** `scenes_read` must equal the scenes whose rows are not `absent` or `unobserved` (the scenes the loader finished reading), checked after the last page and before the first page is
  read again; `unknown_scene` must be 0 and unchanged on every page (a nonzero count is malformed, not a note); generation, digest and `scenes_read` must be the same on every page.
* **Row domains from the emitter's own states.** `listed`: 1..4096 ids, `filled` = `count`, allocation at least `count` (exactly `count` for a declared `file` list); `empty`: no count, allocation or filled entry;
  `absent` and `unobserved`: no source and no content; every other outcome has a source. Nothing a valid retail list could not meet is required; an unsupported outcome is still written and NOT MEASURED.
* **Clock.** Read before anything is asked and after the last answer: a table with finite single-precision `elapsed_seconds` and `time_of_day_seconds` and a whole 32-bit `day`; a missing, text, NaN,
  infinite, fractional or out-of-range value is malformed before the next engine call, and both readings must be equal. The checked values are written. No wall clock or temporal oracle is added.
* **Tests.** A coherent 99-scene multi-page capture (96 + 3 scenes) passes; damaged later pages (unknown scene, scenes read, digest, generation, echo, order, missing row, incoherent row), headers whose total
  the rows do not show, a first page that saw an unknown scene, the row-domain forms and the bad or moved clocks are malformed or not a success and stop at the first bad evidence.
* **Digest scope, stated honestly.** The page digest covers the recorded state and the verified list projection (status, count, filled entries, ids), not every emitted field (source, allocation and success flag
  are not in it); "nothing moved" rests on that digest, the byte-for-byte re-read of the first page and the clock, not on a full-row or table invariance claim. This also qualifies the entry below.
* **Why this characterisation matters (Root's source proof, `model-animation-required-consumer-source-abfda86326-20261101.json`):** `en_anim.c` `set_local_entity_id_number` derives the digit count and
  modulo from the animation membership, seeding the entity id number and campaign significant digits. No metadata ownership has moved; raw legacy ids stay private to this fixture, and the shipping protocol
  is unchanged.
* **Unchanged commands:** `tools/build-windows-model-fixture.sh target/model-fixture/<dir>`, then in a fresh process per scenario `eech-world lua/model-animation-characterization.lua root=<fresh retail root>
  out=<new file> scenario=lebanon_retail` and again with `georgia_retail`. Task50 and D3 (metadata, surfaces, normals, animation lists, hierarchy, viewpoint, device, muzzle, target-point, Task49 collision) and
  Task51's ordinary 78 shipping run remain open; D4 and D5 are not started.

# D3 four-buffer cut: the finite gate is OPEN on three findings (review `7f220c49`); the corrected source is UNBUILT (1 November 2026)

The Root's actuals at `ccc452982a` (their OWN source; not relabelled for the fixed source): default 569/0 and combined 569/0, fmt and both strict 0; Windows combined 0; six controls with all four
Core buffers 0 over 11442 rows (10252 with metadata), 32 cached and 0 uncached horizon images before the REAL uncached call, whose Engine fatal was caught; Georgia 704 and Lebanon 528 files literal
to `9e4`; the short recording literal to `80fd`; the withheld route node refused. They stay valid for the files they measured. The independent review
`independent-model-four-buffer-cut-source-actual-ccc452982a-20261101.json` (SHA256 `7f220c49d8edad4fbabf004390f3a4e760d264a26eb48e8091e9ecc83806d07b`) keeps the finite gate OPEN on three
findings, all closed in SOURCE here and not yet built or run; no claim that every legacy reader is safe until the review of this delta.

* **P1, compiled aliased readers.** `3dtrans.c` (`transform_3d_object`, `transform_flat_3d_object`, `transform_unclipped_3d_object`, `transform_unclipped_flat_3d_object` read `object->points`;
  `transform_3d_object_surface` and `transform_unclipped_3d_object_surface` also read `current_object_3d_surface_point_list`) are called by `draw_wbuffered_3d_object` and its siblings BEFORE the
  guarded face heads, and by terrain and explosion drawing. Added named heads (`LR-reader-*`, 26 new, 66 in all): the scene funnel (`draw_3d_scene`, `draw_normal_scene_objects`,
  `draw_transparent_scene_objects`), the four object draws and three sub-object draws (`3dobjdrw.c`, `3dobjdrz.c`, `3dobjdrh.c`), the six translucent entries, the six transforms and
  `illuminate_3d_object_surface` (its alias is the retained NORMAL list, not a detached buffer; it is stopped as part of the visual family, not as a detached reader), and a leaf with no caller.
  Coverage is a one-off dominance scan over the compiled `modules/3d` callers, function-pointer tables included: of 41 unguarded functions that mention a buffer or an alias, 35 are dominated by a
  head and 6 are not and are explained (the horizon decode after the `LU1` stop, the loader's own NULL assignments and frees, the host-patched collision accessor, and a shadow-visibility local box).
  `3dline.c`, `3dstrail.c` and the terrain files use other structs' `points`. Not a claim about reachability.
* **P2, loader I/O.** The new skips no longer `fseek`: `campaign_model_loader_skip` reads past each region through a 4096-byte buffer with checked counts (64-bit byte count, no narrowing, no Windows
  32-bit `long`), and a short read stops by name (`[model-provider:object.loader]`); the custom polygon-face chunks must be read in full or stop by name (`campaign_model_loader_short`). Valid files
  read the same bytes in the same order (the original read them too), so counts, offsets, texture overrides, the validation phase, FP and RNG are unchanged. The legacy header reads stay unchecked.
* **P2, witness.** `lua/model-horizon-control.lua` now parses the exact C grammar of all three counters rows (names, field order, decimal integers within 0..2147483647, a deliberate bound for
  the Windows 32-bit `long` and Lua doubles), checks coherence (`metadata_rows` and each buffer count not above `rows`, `presence_mismatches` not above `presence_checked`), does NOT launch the
  unsupported reader when the residency or cache evidence is invalid, and accepts the stop only as the whole first line of the Engine-owned fatal error (prefix, named marker, operation and
  reason, equal to the actual CCC primary line). Tests add damaged forms of the first row and of the cause, and tie the driver's grammar and text to the C and the staged guard. The comment that
  claimed feature absence is known before boot is corrected (capability is checked after the boot), and the uncached stop is described as before TGA and geometry work, after the cached `.bin` lookup.
  Source-unit stand-ins are not native proof.
* **Unchanged:** `models.rs`, `model_route.rs`, `model_host.rs`, `model_step.c` and every route, provider, ABI and FP path are as at `c88e479c7`; the cached horizon branch and the fixture counters are
  as they were. 86 staged loader patches (20 loader, face and horizon patches and 66 heads) find their strict counts against the real sources in staging order. No build, test or native run.
  Metadata, surfaces, normals, the animation lists and the hierarchy stay in Core; viewpoint, device, muzzle, target-point services and Task49 collision remain open D3 work. Task51's ordinary
  78 original shipping processes stay LIVE (the 8th check at 21:29: all four prefixes exact, PARTIAL); no 40-check, all-eight or Task closure. The Root's separate path-audit addendum is theirs.

  native witness of one NEW head, not built here, would call the real `transform_3d_object` with all-NULL arguments from a fixture entry: the head stops before any argument is used.

# D3 loader detachment, first batch: three geometry buffers leave Core (1 November 2026): SUPERSEDED by the entry above; SOURCE ONLY; NOT BUILT, NOT RUN

Starting from `c88e479c7`. Under EEDCS-50 the Core loaders no longer hold three of the four physical geometry buffers, in every build with the host route parser (not `model-fixture`, not `route-original`, which keep the original loader):

* **Moved:** `points`, the face point references (`object_faces_point_plain_list`) and the surface-point references (`surface_points`). The common loader does not map `3dobjs.pts` / `3dobjs.sp` or allocate the face
  point list and skips the face-reference bytes by their exact count; the custom `.EEO` loader skips all three streams by their exact byte counts, so every later read, the polygon-face validation and the texture
  overrides run as before. Deinit already tolerates NULL and frees no host buffer.
* **Still true file metadata in Core (not faked, not moved yet):** every count, both bounds, the radius, surface descriptors and their texture/animation indices, normals, texture points, and the whole scene and
  hierarchy load including `add_animation_object` (inputs and order unchanged). **`faces` stays resident:** the Mi-24 HUD (`hi_hud.c:342`, `initialise_hind_hud`) sums the polygon point counts and no host service
  answers that yet; this is the exact dependency, so the detachment is NOT complete. Hierarchy, viewpoint, device, muzzle and target-point services, Task49's collision scans and transforms, and the animation-list
  characterisation (not covered by any closed capture) remain open D3 work. Nothing here is D4 or D5.
* **Readers resolved from the source:** the horizon's points (`3dhoriz.c` `add_3d_horizon_image`, reached at boot from `3d_init.c`) and the cockpit display poly-lines (`3dobjdb.c` `get_object_3d_poly_line_data`; the
  Comanche, Hokum and Ka-50 MFDs) now ask the host through the existing route cursors with kind 0, the scene's main-object role (`models.rs` `route_target`; the echoed kind name is empty; the arithmetic is the same
  float expressions, so the horizon and poly-line values should be bit-equal, which is NOT yet measured). The collision triangle and the route parser were already the host's. 36 renderer / device entries that
  dereference the buffers (draw, transform, shadow, explosion, cloud, horizon and the wface leaves; `LR-reader-*`) stop at their head with `[model-provider:object.geometry]`. Whether any of them is reachable in a
  hosted engine is not established (compiled presence is not dormancy): a run that reaches one fails by name. No existing fixture entry calls one, so that marker has no control yet.
* **Diagnostic:** the model-host fixture's counters entry now also reports `model-core-residency rows= metadata_rows= points= faces= point_refs= surface_points=` over the actual rows. Expected at the next native
  boot: `points`, `point_refs` and `surface_points` 0, `metadata_rows` and `faces` nonzero (faces is the reported residue).
* **Static checks only:** all 52 `LOADER_HOST_PATCHES` find their strict counts against the real sources in the order `main.rs` applies them (PATCHES, MODEL_HOST, ROUTE_HOST, LOADER_HOST), 16 loader / reader patches
  and 36 renderer heads. No build, compile, test, engine or native run, no Tacview claim; the unit test for kind 0 (`model_route.rs`) was written, not run. The Root freezes and builds one pin, then runs the host
  Lua, native boot with the residency line, the horizon and poly-line values against the original, the refusal, and the deterministic captures and short bytes. Task50 and D3 remain In Progress.

# D3 host route boundary: popread consumers of the position table (1 November 2026): SOURCE ONLY; NOT BUILT, NOT RUN

The Root's source inspection found one more required consumer gap: `popread.c` `insert_airport_fixedwing_routes` (and the other airfield insertion functions) read `route_waypoint_positions[current_route_waypoint_offset]`
from the deepest depth (`number_of_route_waypoint_positions - 1`) and step the offset down by fixed amounts (decrements at 2920, 2950, 2964 and so on), so a route with too few depths reads index -1 and below.
Correction (staged patches H19/H20 and `campaign_model_route_position_index`, marker `[model-provider:route.position]`): all 205 occurrences of that indexed expression in `popread.c` (the `WAYPOINT_ALTITUDE` macro is one of them)
now index through a check that returns the same index, once per expression and before anything is read, and stops the engine when the index is outside `[0, number_of_route_waypoint_positions)` or the table does not exist. Nothing
is clamped, defaulted or invented and no minimum depth per route kind is introduced. The six `route_waypoint_positions[count]` troop-route copies are bounded by the table's own count and are not wrapped; routegen.c's own
table writes are untouched. Original-profile build unchanged. Static check only: all 23 named routegen.c/popread.c host patches apply with their counts against the real sources in sequence (H20: 205 expected, 205 found, 0
unwrapped, 6 `[count]` left), and a scan of the 12 offset loops found no read that uses a loop's exit index except the intended floor (offset 3 after the `> 3` taxi loop); no build, test or run.

Control correction for the Root: with the earlier `route.start` guard a connected pure-black TWO-node route no longer reaches `popread` (both nodes are starts, none on a green face, so the old code's uninitialised
`primary_start_index` is now refused as `route.start`). The smallest route that passes the parser is THREE nodes: black start-middle, green middle-leaf (depth count 3). Give landing AND take-off that same answer with
identical coordinates (the take-off's leaf is within 3.0 of the single slot, so no swap). `validate_airport_links` (landing parse, slots, take-off parse) completes; the insertion then reads offsets 2, 1, 0 and the decrement
at 2964 makes it -1, so the expected result is `route.position` for depth -1 of a 3-entry table, before any read at -1. Run on the corrected build only. Together with the 24-tree swap control already given (`route.swap`),
these are source-derived inputs, not executed anywhere. Task50 and D3 remain In Progress; no finite or whole closure. The Root's `50e7f9d85` actuals (704/528 and repeats, short campaign, four topology controls, def6 FP) keep their
own source identity and are not proof of this change.

# D3 host route boundary: one residual P1, the end-slot swap's resolved indices (1 November 2026): SOURCE ONLY; NOT BUILT, NOT RUN

Review `independent-model-host-route-guard-actual-50e7f9d85-20261101.json` (SHA256 `583c29a2d09b5406d9ba976de665a855b22daac98b91a2e05be33a5a7841c2c2`, source `50e7f9d8578c7d283b6a47f01a0e2d09e24cfd2f`) closed the
other new guards and left ENV-HOST-ROUTE-BOUNDARY-2 (P1): `swap_waypoint_nodes` (the end-slot matching's swap) resolves table indices by its own searches and writes through them. Its tree swap searches
the start table from entry 1, so a swap that selects the primary tree (entry 0, the `node_index` 0 leaf) resolves to the logical start count; with 24 starts that is `waypoint_starting_nodes[24]`, and
only the original `ASSERT` stood before the write (the 4096-pass limit comes too late). Its same-parent swap reads two indices that are assigned only if the search finds the child.

Correction (staged patches H16..H18 and `campaign_model_route_swap_index`, marker `[model-provider:route.swap]`): H18 checks both resolved start-table indices (valid: 1 .. logical start count - 1, and inside the
24 entries) immediately BEFORE the original `ASSERT` and before either write; H16 initialises the two child indices to -1 and H17 checks both against the parent's logical child count (and the 24-entry table)
before the first write. The swap is not skipped, redirected or repaired: index 0 is still not searched for, partners are not chosen differently, and every swap the original takes runs the same statements in
the same order. Original-profile parser, provider, store, kernel, ABI, FP and earlier guards are untouched. Static check only: all 18 `routegen.c` host patches find their expected counts against the real
source in sequence, and the candidate fixture's R1/R2/R6 afterwards; no staging build, compile, test, engine or native run. The Root's own actuals at `50e7f9d85` (healthy captures, repeats, four
malformed-topology controls, the short campaign) are those of that identity and do not prove this correction.

One source-derived input recipe for the Root's guarded run (read from `popread.c` 2296..2360 and `routegen.c`, NOT executed anywhere): the campaign parses the fixed-wing landing route with no slots, takes the
absolute positions of its deepest-level nodes in `node_index` order as `matching_slots`, then parses the take-off route of the same airfield with them. Give BOTH routes the same 24 independent trees (a black
edge start-middle and a green edge middle-leaf: 24 starts, 72 nodes, depth 2, one `node_index` k per tree), the landing leaves at distinct positions more than 3.0 apart (slot k), and the take-off leaves the same
except that tree 0's and tree 1's leaf positions are exchanged. Then `match_end_slots` meets slot 0 with the tree-0 leaf (`node_index` 0) out of range, finds tree 1's leaf in range, `swap_waypoint_nodes` climbs both
to their roots (common parent NULL), searches the start table from 1: tree 1's root resolves, tree 0's does not (24). The corrected parser must stop with `route.swap` before any write. Run it only on the corrected build.
Task50 and D3 remain In Progress; the loader detachment is the next real work after the finite boundary is closed.

# D3 host route boundary guards: source-only follow-up to `8d1c79c8d` (1 November 2026): NOT BUILT, NOT RUN

Root's source read found a regression in the new progress guard: `set_node_indices` runs once from the parse and again from every end-slot swap (`swap_waypoint_nodes`, after it has invalidated the
indices), but the guard's count was reset only per parse, so a later invocation could legitimately start with as many nodes unnumbered as the last one ended with and be refused. H11b now begins the
count at the entry of EVERY `set_node_indices` invocation (`campaign_model_route_progress_begin`); no state crosses invocations. The other progress loops each run once per parse (primary, remaining,
and one position loop per depth, each its own phase) and the parse resets them. H7 now puts the named start-capacity check BEFORE the original `ASSERT`. `ROUTE_MATCH_PASSES` 4096 is documented as an
explicit work limit of the host boundary, not derived from the original and not proof that a matching reaching it would never settle: a host route beyond it is unsupported and unmeasured, and a
baseline route reaching it would be a regression. Static check only: all 15 `routegen.c` host patches find their expected counts against the real source in sequence, and the candidate fixture's R1/R2/R6
afterwards; no staging build, compile, test, engine or native run. The Root's finite actuals remain those of `def6a9bde`; nothing here is acceptance of the guards. Task50 and D3 remain In Progress.

# D3 host route boundary: topology guards and cleanup (1 November 2026): SOURCE ONLY; NOT BUILT, NOT RUN

Review `independent-model-host-route-source-actual-def6a9bde-20261101.json` (SHA256 `63027184fff24cd2d2d774aaef1a4d19d089320eff3658b621db3300e041b293`, source `def6a9bdec11e5f8a27451d8057162a34f340693`) found no
blocking issue in the finite seam's data path but two source findings, both addressed here:

* ENV-HOST-ROUTE-BOUNDARY-1 (P1): a route of finite nodes and in-range endpoints could still overrun the parser's 24-entry start, child or parent tables or leave a loop with nothing changing.
  `build/patches.rs` H6..H15 and `mb_hostmodel.c` now stop the hosted parser, before the write or the next pass, with `[model-provider:route.starts|links|progress|start|match]`
  (`docs/m5-route-characterization.md`, "Topology guards"). Valid routes run the unchanged statements in the unchanged order; the original-profile parser, the provider, store, kernel and fixture
  harness are not touched.
* ENV-HOST-ROUTE-CLEANUP-1 (P2): H5b removes the `xmax`/`ymax`/`zmax` setup H5 left unused in the hosted parser; `campaign_model_route_probe` is now compiled only under `EECH_MODEL_HOST_FIXTURE`.

Limits: only a static check was made, that each routegen.c host patch finds its expected count against the real source in sequence, including the candidate fixture's R1/R2/R6 afterwards; no staging build, compile,
test, engine or native run, and the malformed shapes have not been run on any binary. The Root's def6 component evidence (Windows combined build, Linux gates, the 16-entry route FP receipt) was measured
WITHOUT these guards; it is not evidence of them. For the Root's exercise through the public provider, expected markers: 13 disjoint black edges on 26 nodes give `route.starts` at the 25th start; one black edge to a
node with 25 green children gives `route.links` (child table); two nodes and one green edge give `route.progress` (primary nodes); a healthy black-then-green route plus a separate red edge gives
`route.progress` (remaining nodes); an empty route (no node) or one whose starts are none of them on a green line face gives `route.start`. Healthy Georgia 704 and Lebanon 528 must be unchanged and
must raise none. Task50 and D3 remain In Progress; D4 and D5 are unstarted. After the finite seam is accepted the next work is the common and custom physical-model loader detachment with the required reader dispositions.

# D3 route observer: two residual P2 findings corrected in source (1 November 2026): SOURCE ONLY, NOT RUN

Base `9c525b42a`. Independent review `independent-model-original-route-correction-source-9c525b42a-20261101.json` (SHA256 `469dc51993d50c47a8b684545d07f88777a59e7996953c48c9af5df21931a216`, re-hashed) left two P2
findings; both are corrected in source and NOTHING of this batch was built or run (the canonical Linux cache and every other build were kept free for the Root's running gates; checks were static: diff review,
hash and width reading against the repository's `max_width = 160`). Not touched, as asked: the native C observer, hooks, patches, production validators and physics, accepted data. The observer's native C/patches/entries
compiled in the Root's clean Windows route-fixture build of `9c525b42a` (reported: tool session 26133, exit 0) but are NOT run and NOT measured; no original retail data exists; the source gate stays false for these two fixes.

* ENV-ROUTE-OUTPUT-2 (`src/exclusive_output.rs`): `close` no longer treats a successful `sync_all` as the close. The `File` is consumed once: the data sync runs, the raw descriptor / handle is then taken out of the `File` and
  released by the platform's own close (`close` / `CloseHandle`), that result is returned, the release is attempted even if the sync failed, and the `File`'s drop therefore never closes a second time. A sync failure, a close
  failure, or both are reported, each named as what it is; the Lua writer still closes the owned output on every exit and keeps the primary driver failure. Exclusive `create_new` is unchanged. No dependency or generic IO layer.
  New unit tests: the sync/close combination rule; the REAL platform close with a value that is not a descriptor / handle (the operating system refuses it); a real file closed once with complete content. The driver
  tests' "close failed" cases remain a Lua stand-in and are documented as only the driver's reaction.
* ENV-ROUTE-PROTOCOL-2 (`route-characterization.lua`): the identity state is the whole token and must equal the literal `unique`, `unknown` or `ambiguous` (`unique-INVALID` and near misses are malformed); a face-local
  reference must be below its surface's point count; the repeated faces of a surface must carry the same colour, flags, polygon bit and point count; and a face-local reference must resolve to the same node wherever it
  recurs within a surface. These are consistency relations among captured records, not a second route solver. Seven new damaged controls extend the coherent-positive-first set (now 37 call controls, with the 14 summary
  controls: 51) plus four near-miss identity states; every earlier row, capacity, join and no-query-after-malformed check, the verbatim text handling, and all earlier controls are unchanged.

Provenance kept at its own identities: the focused checks of `9c525b42a` are WORKING-TREE checks (the five listed blob hashes match the committed files, but they are not a frozen or canonical run); the old repaired-harness
result stands as 4 passed / 1 failed (the false acceptance), and the old red raw logs record `docker-exit=0` only, so no numeric cargo exit is claimed for them (noted in the retained evidence file). The old `69ef` red text is

# D3 route observer: four independent source findings corrected (1 November 2026): SOURCE + LOCAL DRIVER CHECKS ONLY; OBSERVER UNBUILT, UNRUN, UNMEASURED

Base `844e8d3b9`. Independent review `independent-model-original-route-source-844e8d3b9-20261101.json` (SHA256 `5174507b6c7f3d97a9a7fde8ee1c1362a19e078ab160c54db3a2f0a36b61be62`, re-hashed) found four P2 groups in the
original route observer of `dc4c7c41b`; all four are corrected in source here. NO original retail data exists yet; the C observer, its patches and the engine entries were NOT compiled or linked in this batch;
no geometry responsibility moved. Details and the grammar: `eech-campaign/docs/m5-route-characterization.md`.

* ENV-ROUTE-CAPTURE-1 (`eech_route_fixture.c`): a call is complete only if every promised cohort was captured whole (requested slots, the faces the raw object's surfaces promise, every final node, every parent and
  child link the final rows count with none clipped or outside the table, every start, every depth with all the offsets its count declares). The route identity is the (scene, kind) of the lookups that returned the raw
  object only if exactly one distinct key did; none is `unknown`, several is `ambiguous`; both are recorded in full and incomplete. The latest matching lookup is no longer taken as evidence. Lookups are kept distinct.
  The original parser statements are untouched (the patches R1..R7b did not change); the checks use the original's own counts.
* ENV-ROUTE-PROTOCOL-1 (`route-characterization.lua`): the driver validates the complete native grammar, bounded exact integers and words, row counts, order, indices, identity and the cross-report joins; separates
  measured / well-formed-but-incomplete (the remaining calls are deliberately still read once each for diagnosis, then the run fails) / malformed (written verbatim, `malformed` + `done complete=false`, the engine is not
  asked again); a failed engine call stops the questions. The old positive (declared geometry, no rows) is replaced by a coherent C-shaped synthetic one; 44 damaged-report controls and drift guards against the C
  source are added. The report text is kept byte for byte. No numerical geometry oracle and no generic parser framework.
* ENV-ROUTE-OUTPUT-1: new `host.create_exclusive` (`src/exclusive_output.rs`, registered in `main.rs`; platform create-new open, atomic, refuses ANY existing target) used by the new driver, with checked write, flush and
  close and the owned output closed on every exit. `json-lines.open` and every existing client are unchanged. No `x` open mode is assumed.
* ENV-ROUTE-PROVENANCE-1: the actual-1399 review hash is `280e8808fc239a717824a939d9fae76f8ed1ef37cc76ece9c174091d9e8e8244` (re-hashed from the report bytes); `97781` is a TOOL SESSION identifier, not a count; the actual strict
  evidence is exactly three commands (cargo fmt, default all-target Clippy, combined model-host + ground + line-of-sight all-target Clippy), each exit 0, with no strict runtime test count (the earlier lines below are corrected).

# D3 documentation residual correction D3-MAPPING-BOOT-1 (DOCUMENTATION ONLY; supersedes the `game.cfg` wording of the entry below; no code, build, test or configuration changed)

Corrects `eech-campaign/docs/d3-environment-time-DCS-mapping.md` for the one finding of `independent-environment-time-DCS-mapping-correction-source-ff6240022-20261101.json` (SHA-256 `56790ccd...16ea`; the earlier two P2 findings are closed there). The boot restriction is now stated as an observed REQUEST SEQUENCE, not as a necessary `game.cfg`: the provider sees only `init_fixed (mode)` and cannot tell whether a campaign file's rain tag 0 (DRY) or the configured GOOD/FAIR/POOR sent it; either compatible fixed request makes the mirror global, after which a RANDOM or missing `game.cfg` issues no `init_random`, so with M = DRY and rain tag 0 such a boot reaches STATIC and is accepted. A RANDOM or missing `game.cfg` fails only when neither source established a compatible fixed sequence; a wrong-mode fixed request and an actual later `init_random` still refuse. No separate configuration pre-validation, boot control or new restriction is added. The sentence about the reference step is qualified: after `init_fixed` the reference step still evolves the wind zone and the gust phase, while the lightning timer is evolved only in the local model branch; the planned frozen F(M) step stays a separate provider behaviour. The M6 matrix wording now names the restricted outcomes only, without implying general reload, second-mission, save or client-state support. C0/F(M), the import acknowledgement, the 10 m steady wind plane, gust and ambient content are unchanged.

# D3 documentation correction: the time/DCS mapping after the independent review of 4d84bb335 (DOCUMENTATION ONLY; no code, build, test, configuration or example file changed)

Corrects `eech-campaign/docs/d3-environment-time-DCS-mapping.md` for the two P2 findings of `independent-environment-time-DCS-mapping-source-4d84bb335-20261101.json` (SHA-256 `3f6aa907...13fc`) and makes the open weather row a concrete bounded plan; the review had `weather_mapping_row_closed=false` and this revision is NOT yet accepted by it. (P2-1) The sampled 100/250/50 ms cases are evidence only, not an input domain: the public u32-millisecond API is unchanged, and any future DCS adapter cadence, range, overflow, stall and refusal policy is a separate design with its own validation. (P2-2) Steady `getWind` is an explicit observation policy and proves no repeatability, determinism or native behaviour; the Core checks only a finite speed and, if asked, a finite three-component vector (no norm join), so a coherent tuple is the planned DCS provider's obligation; the weapon-step wind lookup is an active host route, the other readers are an inventory, not proof of reachability. (Weather plan, the technical lead's choice) A RESTRICTED STATIC AUTHORED WEATHER COMPATIBILITY PLAN, legacy compatibility data and not a precipitation observation, with the planned DCS provider kept distinct from the unchanged reference provider: mode M, equality-only pairing, the exact creation-default tuple C0 and F(M) (only local flag, current and target mode, transitional status change), a boot/import/reply table in which import only ACKNOWLEDGES while init_random/init_fixed/export/step refresh the mirror, a static step that is not the reference step (the reference step still drifts wind), lightning_add refused and an empty lightning_update queue, and ONE wind plane (terrain height + 10 m, one steady sample per request, vertical component 0, float32 narrowing and overflow refusal, unity gust, ambient from the same plane with validated authored thresholds). The exact source obstacle is named, not hidden: both campaign parsers call init_random unconditionally at session creation, so refusing every init_random would refuse every boot; the plan tolerates exactly that first request as a no-effect request (answers C0, arms a fixed-weather requirement) and refuses every other one. The only existing route to a configured fixed weather is the persisted `game.cfg` campaign weather read at boot; no engine config, argument or INI key sets it. Native questions (mission-data availability, static truth, real-mission pairing, boot/second-mission/save/client matrix, wind axes/units/precision, callback pause/rounding) stay M6 checks.

# D3 documentation: time ownership and the DCS API / data mapping of the environment services (SOURCE DISPOSITION ONLY; after 78fe5efdb; no code, build, test or configuration changed)

New `eech-campaign/docs/d3-environment-time-DCS-mapping.md` (read from source `78fe5efdb`; Root owns review, gates and delivery). (1) TIME, audited in the source: the public `engine:frame` input is a caller-supplied unsigned 32-bit millisecond count; the `timeGetTime` mirror starts from a function of the random seed (it feeds the shared random stream, so a DCS host supplies only the per-frame increment); the update work runs BEFORE the mirror advances and the next delta is computed, so every frame uses the previous frame's delta, starting at 0.1; the unlocked-rate substeps are a derived cache and scheduling (outer delta restored, reciprocal and history written), not a physical clock. Campaign elapsed time, the calendar, skip-night, timers and scheduling stay Core. The closed positive variable-frame comparison and the wall-calendar classification are reused as Root reported them, bounded to their measured cases; no zero, locked-rate, wrap, pause, time-acceleration, M6 or full floating-point coverage is added. (2) DCS MAPPING from the pinned declarations (`tslua-dcs` 1179554f, `timer.ts`, `atmosphere.ts`, `land.ts` and the generated DCS 2.9.29.27468 data shape `env.export.ts`, with their blobs and SHA-256 in the document): names and types only, never native behaviour, numeric flag meanings or a captured mission. (3) WEATHER, the remaining needed row: a proposed static, authored, paired weather contract that names exactly one EECH mode (DRY, LIGHT_RAIN, HEAVY_RAIN or SNOW), compares the paired `env.mission` values for EQUALITY only, answers the existing `weather` operation as a static global (no zone, ring, transition or lightning) and refuses by name `init_random`, a different `init_fixed` and an import that is neither the session-creation default nor the authored state; dynamic weather cannot be detected from the pinned files and is a declared restriction, not a check; unknowns (numeric meanings, units, whether `env.mission` is readable or live, run-time weather change) are named and not fabricated. Wind is the only weather quantity with a pinned DCS call (`getWind` first, turbulence a reviewed per-consumer choice, sample point and axes an M6 check). (4) The extracted services, the sound backend (snd_data.c's retained loader bodies stay compiled behind the unsupported application flag and would do file and header work if it were true, so the loaders are NOT said to be absent), and the Tasks 49/50 terrain, model and spatial dependencies are covered at a high level without copying their contracts or deferring required physics to D5. Reported by Root at 78 (not reproduced here): the corrected source review clear; the default Core archive and `se_snd.o` actually audited; the first candidate 23 and 24 controls stopped by name; the unarmed 120 still running. Pending: the whole controls, the default actual independent review, shipping 40 and all 8, current CI, final review and delivery; whole Task 51, Tasks 49 and 50, D3, the M6 checks and the weather contract's implementation remain open; D4 and D5 are unstarted. No approval, merge or acceptance is claimed.

# D3 candidate: the legacy native sound backend leaves the campaign Core; every live native-handle request stops by name (SOURCE ONLY, UNBUILT, UNRUN, UNREVIEWED; after 2e2fd548a)

ORIGINAL CHARACTERIZATION CLOSED (reported by Root and the same independent reviewer, not reproduced): `independent-environment-remaining-sound-original-actual-2e2fd548a-20261101.json` (findings [], `finite_remaining_sound_original_characterization_closed=true`; Root corroboration SHA256 `b54f2ecd…30fa`): the first native 2e2fd548a controls 23 and 24 and their controllers 0, 4 complete 1179-file input checks 0, 73 payloads and the driver unchanged, source review `352e813e…75a5` clear, Linux 4 plus format and strict 0, Windows 4 actual 0, the ordinary native rebuild 0; raw outside Git. Case 23: the real pause returned 1, three updates host-counted the invalid expiry, the original released the handle, cleared the entity's handle and unlinked it, and the entity stayed live. Case 24: the assertion prerequisite was logging with debug_log 0; before playing 1, paused 1, volume -1 and afterwards playing 1, paused 0, volume 150, with set volume, get playing and play attributed by source order plus measured flags; the fixture released, detached and zeroed. The record is test-owned controlled state: not a constructor product, a backend, a playing buffer, a device or audio; scan and stop, full floating point, RNG, Tacview and shipping are not claimed. The earlier closed fd16, 3ff, parent, time and ground originals are reused at their identities; no original is rerun.

WHAT THIS CANDIDATE CHANGES (TL needed-only scope). The five physical sound sources `modules/sound/{dirsound,dsound,dsound3d,soundhlp,soundman}.c` are no longer in the campaign build's `sources.txt` (the original game still builds them through its own Makefile). In their place ONE small explicit boundary, `aphavoc/source/appsound/snd_nobackend.c`, under the unchanged declarations of `modules/sound/soundman.h`: (1) the no-backend ANSWERS the legacy callers already handled when the sound system was not initialised: `initialise_sound_system` FALSE, `get_sound_system_devices` FALSE (the existing empty-device fallback of `op_snd.c` and `eechini.c`), `create_single` and `create_sequenced` NULL, `get_first_system_sound_effect` NULL, `is_source_sound_sample_valid` FALSE, `deinitialise_sound_system` empty, `get_sound_system_paused` the unset flag, and `pause_sound_system` and `continue_sound_system` still pause and unpause the CD music exactly as the legacy functions did when uninitialised; `sound_system_initialised` and `sound_system_paused` are the boundary's two FALSE flags (the observer reads them). (2) Everything that asks a native HANDLE to do something (get playing, destroy, user data, play, play sequence, pause, continue, next, volume, position, pitch) stops the engine with `[env-sound:unsupported_native_handle]`, and everything that asks the SAMPLE STORE, the listener or the handle table (allocate, deallocate, create, destroy and load a sample, destroy all, update, orient the listener) with `[env-sound:unsupported_native_backend]`, before any native query, load, playback, destruction or state change; a made-up status or success is never returned for a live request and nothing is dropped. (3) One more refusal at the semantic lifetime boundary: `campaign_sound_lifetime_update` (`ss_hostenv.c`) refuses, after validating the host's answer and BEFORE writing the lifetimes, the host's kill or unlink decision for an effect that holds a handle, with the same handle tag, so the invalid-effect unlink and the kill never commit an unlink before the destroy; the valid-expiry status request keeps its own closed refusal `[env-sound:unsupported_handle_status]`. Native handle pointers and layouts stay private: nothing here reads or writes one. Sample loading is audited and not hidden behind the false capability: `load_application_sound_samples` and `destroy_application_sound_samples` run only when `application_sound_system_enabled` is TRUE, the host's availability is always FALSE (`campaign_sound_backend` refuses an "available" answer by name), and if they were ever reached `allocate_source_sound_samples` and `create_source_sound_sample` would stop by name before the first `fread`. The 14 consumers of the audit (`se_snd.c` four early destroys, the volume-too-low destroy and the create, set and play calls; `se_dstry.c`; both `se_updt.c` invalid branches; the six family destroys; `en_snd.c` scan, stop and the resumed-parent caller; `snd_init.c`; `op_snd.c`; `eechini.c`) need no per-consumer guard: every one reaches this one boundary or the lifetime wrapper. Unchanged: update, RNG and floating point order, the original game's `#else` paths, the no-handle and default behaviour, the host's lifetime arithmetic, the Core entity, child, pause and resume, network and speech bookkeeping, and `eech_com_null.c` and the compat headers.

ENTRY STOP, BEFORE THE PHYSICAL MIXER (independent source review ENV-SOUND-DETACH-1 of 6a7203698, corrected). The adapter's refusal of a volume change is too late to be the evidence that the physical playback left the Core: the SoundEffect dispatch body (`se_snd.c play_local_sound`) computed the range attenuation, the amplification (and, with the on-by-default radio-noise option, one `frand1`), the channel, canopy and interior mixing and the integer volume before it reached any handle call, so public case 24 would still have run that solver in Core. In the hosted campaign build the body now keeps the Core policy gates (the speech and effect options, the interior and exterior locality, the broken radio and the dead effect, whose handle destroys still reach the adapter's refusal) and then DECIDES AT ENTRY: an effect that holds a native handle stops by name, `[env-sound:unsupported_native_handle]`, before any range, volume, entropy, channel or native call; an effect that holds none is an explicitly EMPTY request (with no backend the creators answer NULL, so the legacy body computed a volume it discarded and returned) and returns without computing it. The physical mixer (range to channel mixing, the create, volume, position, pitch, status and play calls, the volume-too-low destroy) is compiled only for the original game (`#else` of `EECH_CAMPAIGN_HOST_ENV`), so it is not a hosted fallback. The one observable difference stated: on the empty route the discarded volume no longer draws the radio-noise `frand1`; that route is not reached by the supported frame (the scan is gated by the host's availability, always FALSE, and the resumed session dispatch is the default no-op); the production gates are bounded facts, not a claim of global dormancy. Case 23's pre-commit unlink stop is unchanged. The adapter's refusals stay as the backstop. Not measured: the 6a7203698 builds Root retained are not the corrected source; the corrected observer and default Core builds, the object audit of `se_snd.o`, the controls and the unarmed comparison are Root's.

FIXTURE AND WITNESS (profile adaptation, stated). The frozen 2e2fd548a native and its historical drivers, expected raw and catalogue 164 are unchanged evidence. In this source the fixture (`eech_env_fixture.c`, comment block before case 21) defines its typed test-owned record array itself (one static record, no device or buffer, TEST-ONLY; it used to be `soundman.c`'s) and releases the record itself on the normal-return path (`env_release_test_record`), because the Core has no native destroy; cases 21 to 24 keep their rows, refusals and the assertion prerequisite, and against this source their expected result is a NAMED STOP (21 and 22 the status stop, 23 the lifetime wrapper's unlink refusal, 24 the ENTRY of the hosted SoundEffect playback request, see the next paragraph, not a handle call). The new small candidate driver `eech-campaign/crates/eech-world/lua/env-sound-native-guard.lua` and `tests/env_sound_native_guard.rs` (2 tests; the `env_sound_handle_guard` shape for cases 23 and 24) accept exactly the engine's strict first-line fatal `runtime error: eech: EECH fatal error: host environment: [env-sound:unsupported_native_handle] ` once, keep the whole fatal text in a private raw before parsing, project only the boundary and fixed words, make no engine call after the poison, create both evidence files exclusively and close both, and have the fail-closed `EECH_SOUND_NATIVE_GUARD_DRIVER_UNDER_TEST` selector. The original drivers and their scripted tests are untouched.

(2) TWO new process-isolated ORIGINAL controls through the real public `env_case` (group 14, observer build): case 23 `handle_invalid_server_unlink` (the real pause on the session invalidates a handle-holding effect, the real server routine counts it down and releases the handle with the real destroy, clears the entity's handle and unlinks it; the valid-expiry status request cannot be made on this route) and case 24 `handle_direct_playback` (the real dispatch on a looping handle-holding effect at the session range 0.0 and the main viewpoint; the real set volume, get playing and play bodies run on the record if the path reaches them). The same test-owned buffer-absent static-array record as 21 and 22 (memory ownership, refusals before any change and the normal-return cleanup are the same; a fatal unwind restores nothing); case 24 starts the record with markers (playing 1, paused 1, volume -1) so the report shows how far the path got, and saves, sets TRUE and restores the sound effects option through the real macro; case 23 refuses when the session already has a child of the paused sub type. The DirectSound entry points the record reaches are the headless null COM stubs (`eech_com_null.c`), so no pointer, vtable, sentinel or device exists; the scan and the stop iteration are NOT reached (the scan needs `get_application_sound_system_status`; the stop walks `get_first_system_sound_effect` whose bound is a static count only `initialise_sound_system` sets) and are reported unexercised, never as passes. The new separate driver `eech-campaign/crates/eech-world/lua/env-sound-route.lua` and `tests/env_sound_route.rs` (4 tests; 21 damaged reports after the coherent positives) keep the report raw and private (it carries the probe's slot and entity words and position words; a safe projection is needed before Git), keep MEASURED flags apart from INFERRED call order, close BOTH exclusive evidence files with failures failing the run, and shadow no name. The originals (`lua/env-sound-handle.lua`, `lua/env-sound-handle-guard.lua`, their tests, cases 21 and 22), all production C and headers and the older cases and oracles are unchanged.

FOR ROOT (nothing run by the author; the only author tools were the pinned offline formatter with the repository `rustfmt.toml`, a fixed point on a second pass, and `git diff --check`). Build with the env-observer and env-fixture features. Guards: `cargo test --locked -p eech-world --test env_sound_route`. Controls, each in its own fresh process and fresh verified root, on the current 6f native or any native of the same sources: `eech-world eech-campaign/crates/eech-world/lua/env-sound-route.lua root=<root> out=<new path> case=23` and `... case=24` (optionally `scenario=georgia_retail`). Preconditions: server comms, a session entity, the sound system uninitialised, handle record 0 free, a free entity slot, and for case 23 no session child of the paused sub type (otherwise the fixture refuses and the driver reports a refusal, not a pass). To be OBSERVED, not assumed: whether the pause invalidated the effect, the unlink and the release in case 23, and in case 24 how far the playback path got (volume, paused and playing flags; a path that returns early or is destroyed by the original is reported as such). Unexercised and stated: the scan, the stop iteration, the early destroys of `se_snd.c` that need other flags or ranges, speech and UI routes, a playing buffer, the original constructor's handle and the network. The obstacle that would have made case 24 unsafe (a NULL buffer reaching a real COM pointer) does not exist in this build because the COM entry points are the headless stubs; if a native shows otherwise the case reports it and nothing is faked. # D3 candidate handle-status guard: the named stop is judged at its structural position, not by substring (P2 corrected; 1 November 2026 UTC; SOURCE ONLY, UNBUILT, UNRUN, UNREVIEWED; guard Lua and test only; after 6f5d522b3)

Finding (independent new-source review, one P2; the reviewer found no extraction flaw): the candidate witness `env-sound-handle-guard.lua` accepted any exception that contained `[env-sound:unsupported_handle_status]` once, so a generic Lua error with that literal, or another native fatal (a provider or callback error) whose detail quotes it, was accepted although the guard claims to accept only the engine's own named stop. A bare tag, a generic Lua error or prefix that quotes it, an unwrapped token, another native fatal that quotes it, a different named stop, the tag after other words, the tag on a later line and a repeated tag all fail with a `witness_failure` reason and no `expected_refusal` or `result`; the complete private raw is unchanged (only the one driver LF delimiter), the JSON holds only the named boundary or the bracketed tag found at that position, and no engine call, readback or cleanup follows the poison. The guard test now models the actual wrapper (with a traceback) in its positives, runs each of the ten negatives with an explicit "ACCEPTED" failure, and has the test-only selector `EECH_SOUND_HANDLE_GUARD_DRIVER_UNDER_TEST` (`var_os`, `PathBuf`, `read_to_string`; default only when the variable is absent; an unreadable, empty, directory or non-Unicode value fails; no environment mutation; the selector's own guard is in the second test).
